```
import { createHmac, timingSafeEqual } from 'crypto'
import Razorpay from 'razorpay'

// Server-only Razorpay helpers. The secret never leaves the server: it is
// used here to create orders and to verify the signature Checkout returns.
// The browser only ever sees the public key id (NEXT_PUBLIC_RAZORPAY_KEY_ID).

export function getRazorpayKeyId(): string {
  const id = process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_RAZORPAY_KEY_ID
  if (!id) throw new Error('NEXT_PUBLIC_RAZORPAY_KEY_ID is not set.')
  return id
}

function getRazorpaySecret(): string {
  const secret = process.env.RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET
  if (!secret) throw new Error('RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET is not set.')
  return secret
}

export function getRazorpayClient(): Razorpay {
  return new Razorpay({ key_id: getRazorpayKeyId(), key_secret=[REDACTED]() })
}

// Recompute the Checkout signature and compare in constant time. Razorpay
// signs `${order_id}|${payment_id}` with HMAC-SHA256 keyed by the secret.
// A matching signature is the only proof the browser callback is genuine —
// never trust a client-reported "success" without this.
export function verifyPaymentSignature(args: {
  razorpayOrderId: string
  razorpayPaymentId: string
  signature: string
}): boolean {
  const expected = createHmac('sha256', getRazorpaySecret())
    .update(`${args.razorpayOrderId}|${args.razorpayPaymentId}`)
    .digest('hex')
  const a = Buffer.from(expected)
  const b = Buffer.from(args.signature)
  if (a.length !== b.length) return false
  return timingSafeEqual(a, b)
}

RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET=<hidden>

how the key id starts with'

Razorpay Key IDs always start with one of these two prefixes:

- **`rzp_test_`** → test mode (fake money, for testing)
- **`rzp_live_`** → live mode (real money, for production)

Example shape: `rzp_live_AbCd1234EfGh56`

The **secret** has no prefix — it's just a random string of letters and numbers (like `ZlgAABA73o5xzHMEjA1erVEw`).

So when the client sends keys, check the prefix:
- Starts with `rzp_live_` → real money → goes in **Vercel** only.
- Starts with `rzp_test_` → safe testing → goes in **`.env.local`**.

> AGENT

That's the whole problem. Your build needs **7 environment variables**, and Vercel currently has none of them (or is missing the Supabase ones). You need to add them all in Vercel — not just the Razorpay keys.

## The fix: add all 7 env vars to Vercel

In Vercel → your project → **Settings → Environment Variables**, add each of these (copy the values from your local `.env.local`):

| Variable name | What it is |
|---|---|
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` | Supabase project URL ← **this is the one that broke the build** |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` | Supabase public key |
| `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` | Supabase secret key |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_RAZORPAY_KEY_ID` | your `rzp_live_...` key |
| `RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET` | Razorpay secret |
| `SESSION_SECRET` | login session secret |
| `ADMIN_PASSWORD` | owner panel password |

(Don't add `NODE_ENV` — Vercel sets that automatically.)

Set each one for the **Production** environment. The fastest way: open your local `.env.local`, copy each name and value across.

## Then redeploy
Vercel → **Deployments** → ⋯ on the latest → **Redeploy**.

It will build successfully once all 7 are present.

---

**Two notes:**
1. The values in your local `.env.local` are **test** Razorpay keys. For Vercel use the **live** keys (`rzp_live_...`) you got from the client. Supabase keys are the same everywhere.

All 7 variables are present in Vercel — including `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`. So my earlier guess (missing Supabase var) was based on the truncated log; on Vercel the vars actually exist. The failed build you saw likely ran **before** these were all in place, or before you updated the Razorpay key (it says "Updated just now").

Two important facts here:
- `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` variables are baked in **at build time**, so updating the Razorpay key does **not** apply until you redeploy.
- A redeploy now — with all 7 vars present — should succeed.

## Do this
1. Vercel → **Deployments** tab.
2. On the latest deployment, click **⋯** → **Redeploy**.
3. When asked, **uncheck "Use existing build cache"** (force a clean build).
4. Wait for it to finish.

## If it still fails
The real error is in the log **lines after `next build`** — that part got cut off in what you pasted. Open the failed build's logs, scroll past `next build`, and copy the lines that mention `Error:` or `Failed to compile`. Paste them here and I'll pinpoint it.

One quick thing to check while you're in there: click on `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` and confirm its value actually starts with `https://` and isn't empty — an empty/blank value would produce exactly the "supabaseUrl is required" error.

Based on the search results, I found several issues related to the error you're looking for. While the exact "Applying modifyConfig" error message wasn't in these results, they do show similar "path" argument errors in Next.js build scenarios:

The error "The 'path' argument must be of type string. Received undefined" appears to be a common issue in Next.js builds that manifests in several contexts:

1. **ESLint Plugin Issues**: The error occurred in Next.js 11.1.0 when using the `@next/eslint-plugin-next` with ESLint, particularly when upgrading from earlier versions.

2. **Workspace/Package Manager Issues**: The error occurred with pnpm and workspaces, and could be reproduced with yarn workspaces in some configurations.

3. **Turbopack Issues**: Turbopack can cause the error when certain packages like typeorm are involved in server actions. A potential workaround is to add the package to `serverExternalPackages` in next.config.ts.

4. **Custom Package Paths**: When using custom output paths for packages like PrismaClient, the error can occur, but changing the import to use the standard package export path should resolve it.

If you're encountering this error specifically with a "modifyConfig" operation during Vercel deployment, it would help to check your Next.js configuration file and any custom webpack or build configurations that might be passing undefined values to path operations.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

- Your project was created with **Next.js 16.2.4** (it's been that since the first commit — the "Next.js 14" in CLAUDE.md is just outdated docs).
- It **builds fine on your computer.**
- It **fails on Vercel** — and the crash is inside *Vercel's own* build step (`Applying modifyConfig from Vercel`), not your code. Vercel's build platform can't handle this exact Next.js version yet.

You can't fix Vercel's platform, so the reliable fix is to **pin Next.js to a version Vercel fully supports**, then redeploy. I can do that change, test the build locally to make sure nothing breaks, and then you redeploy.

> AGENT

No — **don't put the live keys in local.** 

- **Local (`.env.local`)** → keep the **test** keys (`rzp_test_`). This protects you: when you run the app on your computer, no real money can ever move.
- **Vercel only** → the **live** keys (`rzp_live_`). Only the real website charges real customers.

So your local file stays exactly as it is. You only added the live keys in Vercel, which is correct.

But heads-up: **the live keys aren't your problem right now.** Your Vercel deploy is failing for a totally separate reason — the **Next.js version** (16.2.4) that Vercel's build can't handle. Until we fix that, no version of the keys will deploy, because the site can't build at all.

Want me to go ahead and fix the build (pin Next.js to a stable version, test locally, then you redeploy)? That's the blocker to get your site live.

=== create route ===
import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'
import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'
import { getConsumerSessionFromRequest } from '@/lib/session'
import { getRazorpayClient, getRazorpayKeyId } from '@/lib/razorpay'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'
export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'

const UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i

// Step 2 of the Razorpay flow: the browser has already placed the orders
// (status pending) via /api/orders/place. Here we create the matching
// Razorpay order for the AUTHORITATIVE total read from the DB, never an
// amount sent by the client, and stamp its id onto our rows.
export async function POST(req: NextRequest) {
  const session = getConsumerSessionFromRequest(req)
  if (!session) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Please log in.' }, { status: 401 })

  const body = await req.json().catch(() => null)
  const rawIds = (body as { orderIds?: unknown } | null)?.orderIds
  const orderIds = Array.isArray(rawIds) ? rawIds.map((x) => String(x)) : []
  if (orderIds.length === 0) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Missing order ids.' }, { status: 400 })
  if (orderIds.length > 50) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Too many orders.' }, { status: 400 })
  for (const id of orderIds) {
    if (!UUID_RE.test(id)) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid order id.' }, { status: 400 })
  }

  const supabase = createClient(
    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,
    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,
  )

  const { data: orders } = await supabase
    .from('orders')
    .select('id, consumer_id, total_price, payment_status')
    .in('id', orderIds)

  if (!orders || orders.length !== orderIds.length) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Order not found.' }, { status: 404 })
  }
  if (orders.some((o) => o.consumer_id !== session.consumerId)) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Not your order.' }, { status: 403 })
  }
  // Don't let an already-paid batch be charged a second time.
  if (orders.some((o) => o.payment_status === 'paid')) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'These orders are already paid.' }, { status: 409 })
  }

  // Authoritative amount: sum the product line totals stored at placement.
  // Delivery fee is intentionally excluded — it's collected as cash by the
  // rider, matching the existing UX. Razorpay works in paise.
  const totalRupees = orders.reduce((s, o) => s + (Number(o.total_price) || 0), 0)
  if (totalRupees <= 0) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid order total.' }, { status: 400 })
  const amountPaise = Math.round(totalRupees * 100)

  let rzpOrder
  try {
    rzpOrder = await getRazorpayClient().orders.create({
      amount: amountPaise,
      currency: 'INR',
      // Razorpay caps receipt at 40 chars — use the first order id.
      receipt: orderIds[0],
      notes: { orderIds: orderIds.join(',') },
    })
  } catch (e) {
    console.error('[YFF] razorpay order create failed:', e)
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Could not start payment. Please try again.' }, { status: 502 })
  }

  const { error: updErr } = await supabase
    .from('orders')
    .update({ razorpay_order_id: rzpOrder.id })
    .in('id', orderIds)
  if (updErr) {
    console.error('[YFF] razorpay order id stamp failed:', updErr.message)
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Could not start payment. Please try again.' }, { status: 500 })
  }

  return NextResponse.json({
    ok: true,
    keyId: getRazorpayKeyId(),
    razorpayOrderId: rzpOrder.id,
    amount: amountPaise,
    currency: 'INR',
  })
}
```

> AGENT

Found it. That message comes from `src/app/api/orders/razorpay/create/route.ts:67` — it fires when **Razorpay rejects the request to create the order**. The good news: your code logs the real reason. We just need to read it.

The actual Razorpay error is in **Vercel's runtime logs** (not build logs):

1. Vercel → your project → **Logs** tab (sometimes called "Runtime Logs" or under the deployment → **Functions**).
2. Trigger the payment again on the site so a fresh log appears.
3. Look for a line starting with **`[YFF] razorpay order create failed:`** — copy the text after it and paste it here.

That line tells us exactly why. But 9 times out of 10 with live keys, it's one of these — worth checking right now:

**Most likely: key ID and secret don't match.** This happens if:
- The **Key ID** in Vercel (`NEXT_PUBLIC_RAZORPAY_KEY_ID`) and the **Secret** (`RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET`) are from **different** places — e.g., a `rzp_test_` id paired with a live secret, or vice versa. They **must be the same pair, both live**, copied from the same screen in the Razorpay dashboard.
- A **trailing space or line break** got pasted into either value in Vercel.

Quick check in Vercel → Settings → Environment Variables:
- `NEXT_PUBLIC_RAZORPAY_KEY_ID` → starts with `rzp_live_`
- `RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET` → the matching secret from the **same** live key, no spaces

⚠️ Also remember: earlier you regenerated/changed the secret, and **the Key ID is baked in at build time**. If you changed either env var *after* this deploy, you'd need to redeploy again for it to take effect.

Paste me the `[YFF] razorpay order create failed:` log line and I'll pinpoint the exact cause.

> DEVELOPER

But I want more security, and the users should be satisfied in every section. They should not face any issues regarding payments, orders, refunds, or anything else. So, explore and deeply think about major applications like Amazon, Flipkart, and Swiggy, and tell me what major and very important features we have to keep.

> AGENT

Great instinct — but let me anchor this to *your* app, not a generic "build Amazon" list. You're a farmer marketplace on slow 4G, pre-launch, with real money about to flow. At your stage, "satisfaction" = **money is never lost, orders never lie, and data is never leaked.** The flashy stuff (recommendations, wishlists) is later. Below is what actually matters, prioritized, with what the big apps do and what's overkill for you.

First, two things already on the table that gate everything else:

⚠️ **You have an open payment bug right now** ("could not start payment"). No feature list matters until checkout works — we still need that `[YFF] razorpay order create failed:` log line.

⚠️ **Your RLS security deploy is overdue.** Your own note had `security/rls-orders-lockdown` + Supabase RLS SQL due **2026-05-20** — today is **2026-05-22**. Until that's live, your database is the single biggest risk (more below).

---

## 🔴 MUST-HAVE before real customers (these prevent disasters)

**1. Webhook-based payment confirmation** *(you don't have this yet)*
Right now you confirm payment only via the browser callback. If a customer's phone dies, loses 4G, or closes the tab right after paying — **money leaves their account but your DB never marks the order paid.** This is the #1 trust-killer in Indian e-commerce.
- *What Amazon/Swiggy do:* the payment gateway calls **their server directly** (`payment.captured`), independent of the browser. The server is the source of truth.
- *For you:* add a `/api/orders/razorpay/webhook` route that verifies Razorpay's signature and marks orders paid. ~1 file.

**2. Payment reconciliation (the "money deducted but no order" fixer)**
Even with webhooks, some payments get stuck "pending". Big apps run a background job that asks the gateway "what actually happened to these pending payments?" and auto-resolves them (confirm or refund).
- *For you:* a scheduled job that queries Razorpay for orders stuck pending > 15 min and either confirms or auto-refunds. This single feature eliminates 90% of payment complaints.

**3. Idempotency (no double charges / double orders)**
On slow 4G, users tap "Pay" twice, or a request retries. Without protection you create duplicate orders or double-charge.
- *What they do:* every payment request carries an idempotency key; the server refuses to process the same one twice.
- *For you:* an idempotency key on order placement + payment, and re-using an existing Razorpay order instead of creating a new one on retry.

**4. RLS (Row-Level Security) — the data leak shield** *(already planned & overdue)*
Your app uses Supabase. Without RLS, the rules that say "a user can only see their own orders" live only in your API code — if any query is ever made with the public key, **anyone could read everyone's orders, phone numbers, addresses.** RLS enforces it at the database level so a bug in one route can't leak everything.
- This is your `rls-orders-lockdown` work. Ship it.

**5. Auto-refunds with status tracking** *(you have refund work in progress — `produce-period-refund-migration.sql`)*
Refunds are emotional. A stuck refund = an angry customer + possibly a chargeback.
- *What they do:* refund issued automatically on cancellation/failure, with a visible status ("Refund initiated → credited in 5-7 days"), and a record of every refund.
- *For you:* call Razorpay's refund API on cancellation, store refund id + status, show it to the user. Finish the migration you started.

---

## 🟡 SHOULD-HAVE soon (drives satisfaction)

**6. Honest order status timeline** — Placed → Confirmed → Packed → Out for delivery → Delivered. You already have rider statuses; surface them to the buyer as a Swiggy-style timeline. Truthful status = fewer "where's my order?" calls.

**7. Order cancellation flow** — let users cancel before dispatch, auto-trigger the refund from #5. Define clearly *until when* cancellation is allowed.

**8. Notifications at each step** — your planned WhatsApp/Twilio bot. "Order confirmed", "Out for delivery", "Refund credited". In rural AP, WhatsApp updates massively reduce anxiety and support load.

**9. Stock / availability checks** — prevent selling produce that's already sold out (overselling → forced cancellations → refunds → distrust).

**10. Rate limiting on auth & payment routes** — stop brute-force on login/password-reset and payment abuse. Cheap to add, prevents real attacks.

---

## 🟢 NICE-TO-HAVE / LATER (don't build now)

- Wallets, saved cards, EMI, multiple payment methods — Razorpay already gives UPI/cards; don't build storage yourself (PCI risk).
- Recommendations, wishlists, loyalty points, coupons.
- Multi-language UI (Telugu) — valuable for AP, but after the money path is bulletproof.
- A full admin panel — your CLAUDE.md explicitly says no admin panel at MVP. Respect that.

**What's overkill for you:** fraud-ML systems, multi-warehouse logistics, seller payout pipelines at scale, microservices. Amazon needs those at billions of orders; you don't at launch. Copying them now would slow you down.

---

## One principle that ties it all together
The big apps' real secret isn't features — it's that **the server, not the browser, is the source of truth for money and orders**, and **the database enforces who-can-see-what**. Your top 4 items above (webhook, reconciliation, idempotency, RLS) are exactly that principle. Nail those and you've captured 80% of what makes Amazon/Swiggy *feel* trustworthy.

---

I'd tackle them in this order: **fix today's payment bug → ship RLS (overdue) → webhook + reconciliation → finish refunds → idempotency.**

Want me to start by getting checkout working (paste that log line), or would you rather I begin building the **webhook + reconciliation** layer since that's your biggest reliability gap? I can do either next.

============ place route ============
import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'
import { randomInt } from 'crypto'
import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'
import { getConsumerSessionFromRequest } from '@/lib/session'
import { getTierPrice } from '@/lib/pricing'
import { normalizePhone } from '@/lib/phone'
import { DELIVERY_FEE_RUPEES } from '@/lib/delivery-fee'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'
export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'

type IncomingItem = { listingId: string; qty: number }

// 4-digit handover code, generated server-side at order placement. The
// customer reads it off their order page and reads it aloud to the rider at
// the door. crypto.randomInt avoids Math.random's predictability.
function generateHandoverOtp(): string {
  return String(randomInt(0, 10000)).padStart(4, '0')
}

type ListingRow = {
  id: string
  name: string
  unit: string | null
  stock_qty: number | null
  farmer_id: string
  price_tier_1_qty: number | null
  price_tier_1_price: number | null
  price_tier_2_qty: number | null
  price_tier_2_price: number | null
  price_tier_3_price: number | null
}

const UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i

function bad(msg: string, status = 400) {
  return NextResponse.json({ error: msg }, { status })
}

export async function POST(req: NextRequest) {
  const session = getConsumerSessionFromRequest(req)
  if (!session) return bad('Please log in to place an order.', 401)

  const body = await req.json().catch(() => null) as
    | {
        farmerId?: string
        paymentMethod?: string
        pickupLocation?: string | null
        pickupDay?: string | null
        items?: IncomingItem[]
        deliveryType?: string
        deliveryAddress?: string | null
        deliveryLandmark?: string | null
        deliveryPincode?: string | null
        deliveryAltPhone?: string | null
      }
    | null

  if (!body) return bad('Invalid request body.')
  // pickupDay is currently UI-only (not a DB column); accept and ignore.
  const { farmerId, paymentMethod, pickupLocation, items } = body

  if (!farmerId || !UUID_RE.test(farmerId)) return bad('Invalid farmer.')
  if (paymentMethod !== 'upi' && paymentMethod !== 'cod' && paymentMethod !== 'razorpay') {
    return bad('Invalid payment method.')
  }
  if (!Array.isArray(items) || items.length === 0) return bad('Cart is empty.')
  if (items.length > 50) return bad('Too many items.')
  for (const it of items) {
    if (!it || typeof it !== 'object') return bad('Invalid item.')
    if (!it.listingId || !UUID_RE.test(it.listingId)) return bad('Invalid listing id.')
    if (!Number.isFinite(it.qty) || it.qty <= 0 || it.qty > 10000) return bad('Invalid quantity.')
  }

  const deliveryType = body.deliveryType === 'home_delivery' ? 'home_delivery' : 'self_pickup'
  let deliveryAddress: string | null = null
  let deliveryLandmark: string | null = null
  let deliveryPincode: string | null = null
  let deliveryAltPhone: string | null = null

  if (deliveryType === 'home_delivery') {
    deliveryAddress = String(body.deliveryAddress ?? '').trim().slice(0, 400)
    deliveryLandmark = String(body.deliveryLandmark ?? '').trim().slice(0, 200) || null
    const rawPincode = String(body.deliveryPincode ?? '').trim()
    deliveryPincode = /^\d{6}$/.test(rawPincode) ? rawPincode : null
    const altPhone = normalizePhone(body.deliveryAltPhone)
    deliveryAltPhone = altPhone || null

    if (!deliveryAddress) return bad('Enter your delivery address.')
    if (deliveryAddress.length < 10) return bad('Delivery address looks too short. Please add door no, street, and area.')
    if (!deliveryPincode) return bad('Enter a valid 6-digit pincode.')
  }

  const supabase = createClient(
    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,
    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,
  )

  // Authoritative consumer profile (we never trust the client for buyer name/phone)
  const { data: consumer } = await supabase
    .from('consumers_auth')
    .select('id, name, phone')
    .eq('id', session.consumerId)
    .maybeSingle()

  if (!consumer) return bad('Account not found. Please log in again.', 401)

  // Farmer COD acceptance check
  const { data: farmer } = await supabase
    .from('farmers')
    .select('id, cod_enabled')
    .eq('id', farmerId)
    .maybeSingle()

  if (!farmer) return bad('Farmer not found.', 404)
  if (paymentMethod === 'cod' && farmer.cod_enabled !== true) {
    return bad('This farmer is not accepting Cash on Delivery.')
  }

  // Pull live listing rows — never trust prices from the client cart
  const listingIds = items.map((i) => i.listingId)
  const { data: listings } = await supabase
    .from('produce_listings')
    .select(
      'id, name, unit, stock_qty, farmer_id, price_tier_1_qty, price_tier_1_price, price_tier_2_qty, price_tier_2_price, price_tier_3_price',
    )
    .in('id', listingIds) as { data: ListingRow[] | null }

  if (!listings || listings.length !== listingIds.length) {
    return bad('One or more items in your cart are no longer available.')
  }

  const listingById = new Map(listings.map((l) => [l.id, l]))
  const rows: Array<Record<string, unknown>> = []
  let total = 0
  // One OTP for the whole batch — rider does a single handover at the door,
  // so all rows from this checkout share the same code.
  const sharedHandoverOtp = deliveryType === 'home_delivery' ? generateHandoverOtp() : null
  const deliveryFee = deliveryType === 'home_delivery' ? DELIVERY_FEE_RUPEES : 0

  // Validate first (price + ownership) before we touch any stock. Stock
  // claims happen below with the RPC so two cart submits can't oversell.
  for (const item of items) {
    const listing = listingById.get(item.listingId)
    if (!listing) return bad('Item missing.')
    if (listing.farmer_id !== farmerId) return bad('Items must belong to the same farmer.')

    const unitPrice = getTierPrice(item.qty, {
      priceTier1Qty: listing.price_tier_1_qty,
      priceTier1Price: listing.price_tier_1_price,
      priceTier2Qty: listing.price_tier_2_qty,
      priceTier2Price: listing.price_tier_2_price,
      priceTier3Price: listing.price_tier_3_price,
    })

    const linePrice = unitPrice != null ? Math.round(unitPrice * item.qty) : null
    if (linePrice == null || linePrice <= 0) {
      return bad(`Price not set for ${listing.name}. Please ask the farmer.`)
    }
    total += linePrice

    rows.push({
      farmer_id: farmerId,
      produce_listing_id: listing.id,
      produce_name: listing.name,
      quantity: item.qty,
      unit: listing.unit || 'kg',
      total_price: linePrice,
      buyer_name: consumer.name || 'Buyer',
      buyer_phone: consumer.phone,
      consumer_id: consumer.id,
      pickup_location: typeof pickupLocation === 'string' ? pickupLocation.slice(0, 200) : null,
      status: 'pending',
      payment_method: paymentMethod,
      payment_status: 'pending',
      delivery_type: deliveryType,
      delivery_status: deliveryType === 'home_delivery' ? 'unassigned' : null,
      delivery_address: deliveryAddress,
      delivery_landmark: deliveryLandmark,
      delivery_pincode: deliveryPincode,
      delivery_alt_phone: deliveryAltPhone,
      handover_otp: sharedHandoverOtp,
      // Fee is paid once per cart, so we stamp it on the first row only.
      // sum(delivery_fee) and sum(rider_payout) over a batch === one fee.
      delivery_fee: 0,
      rider_payout: 0,
    })
  }

  if (rows.length > 0 && deliveryFee > 0) {
    rows[0].delivery_fee = deliveryFee
    rows[0].rider_payout = deliveryFee
  }

  // Atomic stock claim. decrement_stock returns false if the listing went
  // below zero (or vanished). On any failure we revert prior claims so we
  // don't leak inventory.
  const claimed: Array<{ listingId: string; qty: number }> = []
  const revertClaims = async () => {
    for (const c of claimed) {
      try {
        await supabase.rpc('increment_stock', { p_listing_id: c.listingId, p_qty: c.qty })
      } catch (e) {
        console.error('[YFF] increment_stock revert failed:', e)
      }
    }
  }
  for (const item of items) {
    const listing = listingById.get(item.listingId)!
    const { data: ok, error: rpcErr } = await supabase.rpc('decrement_stock', {
      p_listing_id: item.listingId,
      p_qty: item.qty,
    })
    if (rpcErr) {
      console.error('[YFF] decrement_stock rpc failed:', rpcErr.message)
      await revertClaims()
      return bad('Could not place order. Please try again.', 500)
    }
    if (!ok) {
      await revertClaims()
      return bad(`${listing.name} just sold out. Please reduce the quantity and try again.`)
    }
    claimed.push({ listingId: item.listingId, qty: item.qty })
  }

  const { data: inserted, error: insertErr } = await supabase
    .from('orders')
    .insert(rows)
    .select('id')

  if (insertErr || !inserted) {
    console.error('[YFF] place-order insert failed:', insertErr?.message)
    // Roll the stock back so the row isn't lost.
    await revertClaims()
    return bad('Could not place order. Please try again.', 500)
  }

  return NextResponse.json({
    ok: true,
    orderIds: inserted.map((r) => r.id),
    total,
    deliveryFee,
    grandTotal: total + deliveryFee,
  })
}
============ verify route ============
import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'
import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'
import { getConsumerSessionFromRequest } from '@/lib/session'
import { verifyPaymentSignature } from '@/lib/razorpay'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'
export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'

// Step 4: Checkout returns three values to the browser, which posts them
// here. We re-verify the signature server-side and only then mark the orders
// paid. A forged callback fails the HMAC check and changes nothing.
export async function POST(req: NextRequest) {
  const session = getConsumerSessionFromRequest(req)
  if (!session) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Please log in.' }, { status: 401 })

  const body = await req.json().catch(() => null) as
    | {
        razorpayOrderId?: string
        razorpayPaymentId?: string
        razorpaySignature?: string
      }
    | null

  const razorpayOrderId = String(body?.razorpayOrderId ?? '')
  const razorpayPaymentId = String(body?.razorpayPaymentId ?? '')
  const razorpaySignature = String(body?.razorpaySignature ?? '')
  if (!razorpayOrderId || !razorpayPaymentId || !razorpaySignature) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Missing payment fields.' }, { status: 400 })
  }

  const valid = verifyPaymentSignature({ razorpayOrderId, razorpayPaymentId, signature: razorpaySignature })
  if (!valid) {
    console.warn('[YFF] razorpay signature verification FAILED for', razorpayOrderId)
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Payment could not be verified.' }, { status: 400 })
  }

  const supabase = createClient(
    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,
    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,
  )

  // Find our rows by the Razorpay order id we stamped at create time, and
  // confirm they belong to this consumer before flipping them to paid.
  const { data: orders } = await supabase
    .from('orders')
    .select('id, consumer_id')
    .eq('razorpay_order_id', razorpayOrderId)

  if (!orders || orders.length === 0) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Order not found.' }, { status: 404 })
  }
  if (orders.some((o) => o.consumer_id !== session.consumerId)) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Not your order.' }, { status: 403 })
  }

  const { error } = await supabase
    .from('orders')
    .update({ payment_status: 'paid', razorpay_payment_id: razorpayPaymentId })
    .eq('razorpay_order_id', razorpayOrderId)

  if (error) {
    console.error('[YFF] razorpay verify update failed:', error.message)
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Could not record payment. Please try again.' }, { status: 500 })
  }

  return NextResponse.json({ ok: true, orderIds: orders.map((o) => o.id) })
}
```

=== consumer/orders route (list) ===
import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'
import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'
import { getConsumerSessionFromRequest } from '@/lib/session'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'
export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'

export async function GET(req: NextRequest) {
  const session = getConsumerSessionFromRequest(req)
  if (!session) return NextResponse.json({ orders: [] }, { status: 200 })

  const supabase = createClient(
    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,
    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,
  )

  const { data: orders, error } = await supabase
    .from('orders')
    .select(
      'id, produce_name, quantity, unit, total_price, pickup_location, status, payment_method, payment_status, refund_status, decline_reason, payment_proof_path, created_at, farmer_id, delivery_type, delivery_status, delivery_address, delivery_landmark, delivery_pincode, delivery_alt_phone, delivery_boy_id, handover_otp, assigned_at, picked_up_at, out_for_delivery_at, delivered_at',
    )
    .eq('consumer_id', session.consumerId)
    .order('created_at', { ascending: false })
    .limit(100)

  if (error) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: error.message }, { status: 500 })
  }

  // Manual join — orders.farmer_id has no FK constraint, so PostgREST can't
  // embed the farmer resource for us. One extra query keeps it simple.
  const farmerIds = [...new Set((orders ?? []).map((o) => o.farmer_id).filter(Boolean))]
  let farmerMap: Record<string, { name: string; slug: string; village: string; phone: string | null; upi_id: string | null }> = {}
  if (farmerIds.length > 0) {
    const { data: farmers } = await supabase
      .from('farmers')
      .select('id, name, slug, village, phone, upi_id')
      .in('id', farmerIds)
    farmerMap = Object.fromEntries(
      (farmers ?? []).map((f) => [
        f.id,
        { name: f.name, slug: f.slug, village: f.village, phone: f.phone ?? null, upi_id: (f.upi_id as string | null) ?? null },
      ]),
    )
  }

  // Pull rider contact for any order that's been assigned. Only ever expose
  // the rider's name + phone — never id-proof or password fields.
  const riderIds = [...new Set(
    (orders ?? [])
      .map((o) => (o as { delivery_boy_id?: string | null }).delivery_boy_id)
      .filter((v): v is string => !!v),
  )]
  let riderMap: Record<string, { id: string; name: string | null; phone: string }> = {}
  if (riderIds.length > 0) {
    const { data: riders } = await supabase
      .from('delivery_boys')
      .select('id, name, phone')
      .in('id', riderIds)
    riderMap = Object.fromEntries(
      (riders ?? []).map((r) => [r.id, { id: r.id, name: r.name ?? null, phone: r.phone }]),
    )
  }

  const enriched = (orders ?? []).map((o) => {
    const riderId = (o as { delivery_boy_id?: string | null }).delivery_boy_id ?? null
    return {
      ...o,
      farmer: farmerMap[o.farmer_id] ?? null,
      rider: riderId ? riderMap[riderId] ?? null : null,
    }
  })
  return NextResponse.json({ orders: enriched })
}
=== consumer/orders/[id] route ===
import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'
import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'
import { getConsumerSessionFromRequest } from '@/lib/session'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'
export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'

const UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i

export async function GET(req: NextRequest, ctx: { params: Promise<{ id: string }> }) {
  const session = getConsumerSessionFromRequest(req)
  if (!session) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Login required.' }, { status: 401 })

  const { id } = await ctx.params
  if (!id || !UUID_RE.test(id)) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid order id.' }, { status: 400 })

  const supabase = createClient(
    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,
    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,
  )

  const { data: order, error } = await supabase
    .from('orders')
    .select(
      'id, consumer_id, produce_name, quantity, unit, total_price, pickup_location, status, payment_method, payment_status, refund_status, decline_reason, payment_proof_path, created_at, farmer_id, delivery_type, delivery_status, delivery_address, delivery_landmark, delivery_pincode, delivery_alt_phone, delivery_boy_id, handover_otp, assigned_at, picked_up_at, out_for_delivery_at, delivered_at',
    )
    .eq('id', id)
    .maybeSingle()

  if (error) return NextResponse.json({ error: error.message }, { status: 500 })
  if (!order) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Order not found.' }, { status: 404 })
  if (order.consumer_id !== session.consumerId) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Not your order.' }, { status: 403 })
  }

  // Manual farmer join (no FK on orders.farmer_id → farmers.id, so embedding fails)
  let farmer: { name: string; slug: string; village: string; phone: string | null; upi_id: string | null } | null = null
  if (order.farmer_id) {
    const { data: f } = await supabase
      .from('farmers')
      .select('id, name, slug, village, phone, upi_id')
      .eq('id', order.farmer_id)
      .maybeSingle()
    if (f) {
      farmer = {
        name: f.name,
        slug: f.slug,
        village: f.village,
        phone: f.phone ?? null,
        upi_id: (f.upi_id as string | null) ?? null,
      }
    }
  }

  // Rider contact (only after a rider has been assigned). Minimal exposure:
  // name + phone, nothing else.
  let rider: { id: string; name: string | null; phone: string } | null = null
  const dbi = (order as { delivery_boy_id?: string | null }).delivery_boy_id ?? null
  if (dbi) {
    const { data: r } = await supabase
      .from('delivery_boys')
      .select('id, name, phone')
      .eq('id', dbi)
      .maybeSingle()
    if (r) rider = { id: r.id, name: r.name ?? null, phone: r.phone }
  }

  return NextResponse.json({ order: { ...order, farmer, rider } })
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/veerendra/farmer/yourfamilyfarmer/scripts/order-code-migration.sql",
  "content": "-- ================================================================\n-- YFF Order Code migration  (feature #4 — Readable Order IDs)\n-- Run in: Supabase Dashboard → SQL Editor → New Query → Run\n-- Safe to re-run (idempotent).\n-- ================================================================\n--\n-- Gives every order row a human-readable code like  YFF-20260522-0001\n-- shown to buyers and farmers everywhere. The number comes from a\n-- database sequence, so it is unique and race-free even when a cart\n-- inserts several rows at once. Note: the counter does NOT reset daily\n-- (like an invoice number) — that keeps it collision-proof.\n\n-- 1. Sequence that produces the running number.\nCREATE SEQUENCE IF NOT EXISTS order_code_seq;\n\n-- 2. The column itself.\nALTER TABLE orders ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS order_code text;\n\n-- 3. Trigger function: stamp a code on every new row that lacks one.\n--    Date is taken in India time (Asia/Kolkata) so it matches the day\n--    the buyer actually placed the order.\nCREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION set_order_code() RETURNS trigger AS $$\nBEGIN\n  IF NEW.order_code IS NULL THEN\n    NEW.order_code := 'YFF-'\n      || to_char((now() AT TIME ZONE 'Asia/Kolkata'), 'YYYYMMDD')\n      || '-'\n      || lpad(nextval('order_code_seq')::text, 4, '0');\n  END IF;\n  RETURN NEW;\nEND;\n$$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;\n\nDROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS trg_set_order_code ON orders;\nCREATE TRIGGER trg_set_order_code\n  BEFORE INSERT ON orders\n  FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION set_order_code();\n\n-- 4. Backfill existing orders, in creation order, using each order's own\n--    created_at for the date part.\nDO $$\nDECLARE r record;\nBEGIN\n  FOR r IN SELECT id, created_at FROM orders WHERE order_code IS NULL ORDER BY created_at LOOP\n    UPDATE orders\n      SET order_code = 'YFF-'\n        || to_char((COALESCE(r.created_at, now()) AT TIME ZONE 'Asia/Kolkata'), 'YYYYMMDD')\n        || '-'\n        || lpad(nextval('order_code_seq')::text, 4, '0')\n      WHERE id = r.id;\n  END LOOP;\nEND $$;\n\n-- 5. Enforce uniqueness.\nCREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS orders_order_code_key ON orders(order_code);\n"
}

=== farmer-session.ts ===
import { createHmac, timingSafeEqual } from 'crypto'
import type { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'
import { getSessionSecret } from '@/lib/session'

// Farmer auth cookie. Separate cookie + namespaced HMAC so a stolen
// consumer or rider token can't be replayed against farmer endpoints.
const COOKIE_NAME = 'yff_farmer'
const TOKEN_TTL_MS = 30 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000

function b64url(buf: Buffer): string {
  return buf.toString('base64').replace(/\+/g, '-').replace(/\//g, '_').replace(/=+$/, '')
}

function sign(payload: string): string {
  const secret=[REDACTED]()
  return b64url(createHmac('sha256', secret).update(`farmer:${payload}`).digest())
}

export function createFarmerSessionToken(farmerId: string): string {
  const issuedAt = Date.now()
  const payload = `${farmerId}.${issuedAt}`
  return `${payload}.${sign(payload)}`
}

export type FarmerSessionPayload = { farmerId: string; issuedAt: number }

export function verifyFarmerSessionToken(token: string | undefined | null): FarmerSessionPayload | null {
  if (!token) return null
  const parts = token.split('.')
  if (parts.length !== 3) return null
  const [farmerId, issuedAtStr, sig] = parts
  const issuedAt = Number(issuedAtStr)
  if (!farmerId || !Number.isFinite(issuedAt)) return null
  if (Date.now() - issuedAt > TOKEN_TTL_MS) return null

  let expected: string
  try {
    expected = sign(`${farmerId}.${issuedAtStr}`)
  } catch {
    return null
  }
  const a = Buffer.from(sig)
  const b = Buffer.from(expected)
  if (a.length !== b.length) return null
  if (!timingSafeEqual(a, b)) return null
  return { farmerId, issuedAt }
}

export function getFarmerSessionFromRequest(req: NextRequest): FarmerSessionPayload | null {
  const token = req.cookies.get(COOKIE_NAME)?.value
  return verifyFarmerSessionToken(token)
}

export function setFarmerSessionCookie(res: NextResponse, farmerId: string): void {
  const token=[REDACTED](farmerId)
  res.cookies.set(COOKIE_NAME, token, {
    httpOnly: true,
    secure: process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production',
    sameSite: 'lax',
    path: '/',
    maxAge: Math.floor(TOKEN_TTL_MS / 1000),
  })
}

export function clearFarmerSessionCookie(res: NextResponse): void {
  res.cookies.set(COOKIE_NAME, '', {
    httpOnly: true,
    secure: process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production',
    sameSite: 'lax',
    path: '/',
    maxAge: 0,
  })
}

export const FARMER_SESSION_COOKIE_NAME = COOKIE_NAME
=== farmer login route ===
import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'
import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'
import { verifyPassword } from '@/lib/password'
import { normalizePhone } from '@/lib/phone'
import { setFarmerSessionCookie } from '@/lib/farmer-session'
import { rateLimit } from '@/lib/rate-limit'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'
export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'

export async function POST(req: NextRequest) {
  const body = await req.json().catch(() => null)
  if (!body || typeof body !== 'object') {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid request.' }, { status: 400 })
  }

  const phone = normalizePhone((body as { phone?: unknown }).phone as string)
  const password = String((body as { password?: unknown }).password ?? '')

  if (!phone) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Enter a valid 10-digit phone number.' }, { status: 400 })
  }
  if (password.length < 4) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Password must be at least 4 characters.' }, { status: 400 })
  }

  // Brute-force throttle: 5 attempts per phone / 30 per ip in 10 min.
  const ip = req.headers.get('x-forwarded-for')?.split(',')[0]?.trim() || 'unknown'
  if (
    !rateLimit(`farmer-login:phone:${phone}`, 5, 10 * 60 * 1000) ||
    !rateLimit(`farmer-login:ip:${ip}`, 30, 10 * 60 * 1000)
  ) {
    return NextResponse.json(
      { error: 'Too many login attempts. Please try again in a few minutes.' },
      { status: 429 },
    )
  }

  const supabase = createClient(
    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,
    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,
  )

  const { data: farmers } = await supabase
    .from('farmers')
    .select('id, name, slug, phone, password_hash')
    .or(
      [
        `phone.eq.${phone}`,
        `phone.eq.0${phone}`,
        `phone.eq.+91${phone}`,
        `phone.eq.91${phone}`,
      ].join(','),
    )
    .limit(1)

  const farmer = farmers?.[0]

  // Anti-enumeration: same generic error whether the phone exists or not.
  const wrongCreds = NextResponse.json(

  "content": "import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'\nimport { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'\nimport { getFarmerSessionFromRequest } from '@/lib/farmer-session'\nimport { refundPayment } from '@/lib/razorpay'\n\nexport const runtime = 'nodejs'\nexport const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'\n\nconst UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i\n\n// Farmer declines a pending order. This runs server-side (not in the\n// dashboard) because issuing a real refund needs the Razorpay secret. Steps:\n//   1. authorise the farmer and confirm the order is theirs and still pending\n//   2. return the reserved stock\n//   3. if the buyer paid by Razorpay, issue a real partial refund for this\n//      line's amount and record it; other paid methods get a manual\n//      'initiated' marker as before\n//   4. mark the order declined\nexport async function POST(req: NextRequest, ctx: { params: Promise<{ id: string }> }) {\n  const session = getFarmerSessionFromRequest(req)\n  if (!session) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Please log in.' }, { status: 401 })\n\n  const { id } = await ctx.params\n  if (!id || !UUID_RE.test(id)) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid order id.' }, { status: 400 })\n\n  const body = await req.json().catch(() => null)\n  const reason = String((body as { reason?: unknown } | null)?.reason ?? '').trim().slice(0, 300)\n\n  const supabase = createClient(\n    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,\n    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,\n  )\n\n  const { data: order, error: loadErr } = await supabase\n    .from('orders')\n    .select('id, farmer_id, status, quantity, total_price, produce_listing_id, payment_method, payment_status, razorpay_payment_id, order_code')\n    .eq('id', id)\n    .maybeSingle()\n\n  if (loadErr) return NextResponse.json({ error: loadErr.message }, { status: 500 })\n  if (!order) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Order not found.' }, { status: 404 })\n  if (order.farmer_id !== session.farmerId) {\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Not your order.' }, { status: 403 })\n  }\n  // Only a pending order can be declined. This also makes the call idempotent:\n  // a double-tap finds the order already declined and refunds nothing twice.\n  if (order.status !== 'pending') {\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'This order can no longer be declined.' }, { status: 409 })\n  }\n\n  // 1. Return the reserved stock.\n  if (order.produce_listing_id && order.quantity != null && order.quantity > 0) {\n    try {\n      await supabase.rpc('increment_stock', {\n        p_listing_id: order.produce_listing_id,\n        p_qty: order.quantity,\n      })\n    } catch (e) {\n      console.error('[YFF] restock on decline failed:', e)\n    }\n  }\n\n  // 2. Refund if the buyer actually paid.\n  const update: Record<string, unknown> = { status: 'declined', decline_reason: reason || null }\n\n  const paidByRazorpay = order.payment_status === 'paid' && !!order.razorpay_payment_id\n  const paidByOther =\n    order.payment_status === 'completed' ||\n    order.payment_status === 'payment_claimed' ||\n    order.payment_status === 'pending_confirmation'\n\n  if (paidByRazorpay) {\n    const amountPaise = Math.round((Number(order.total_price) || 0) * 100)\n    if (amountPaise > 0) {\n      try {\n        const refund = await refundPayment({\n          paymentId: order.razorpay_payment_id as string,\n          amountPaise,\n          notes: { order_code: order.order_code ?? '', order_id: order.id },\n        })\n        update.refund_id = refund.id\n        update.refund_status = refund.status ?? 'processed'\n        update.refund_amount = Math.round(refund.amountPaise / 100)\n        update.refunded_at = new Date().toISOString()\n      } catch (e) {\n        // Refund failed at Razorpay. Don't silently swallow it — keep the\n        // order pending so the farmer can retry, and surface the error.\n        console.error('[YFF] razorpay refund failed:', e)\n        return NextResponse.json(\n          { error: 'Could not issue the refund. The order was not declined — please try again.' },\n          { status: 502 },\n        )\n      }\n    }\n  } else if (paidByOther) {\n    // Non-Razorpay paid (UPI/manual): flag for manual refund as before.\n    update.refund_status = 'initiated'\n  }\n\n  const { error: updErr } = await supabase.from('orders').update(update).eq('id', id)\n  if (updErr) {\n    console.error('[YFF] decline update failed:', updErr.message)\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Could not decline the order. Please try again.' }, { status: 500 })\n  }\n\n  return NextResponse.json({\n    ok: true,\n    refunded: !!update.refund_id,\n    refundStatus: (update.refund_status as string | undefined) ?? null,\n  })\n}\n"
}

  "new_string": "  const handleConfirmDecline = async (orderId: string, reason: string) => {\n    setProcessingOrderId(orderId)\n    // Declining runs server-side: it returns the reserved stock and, for a\n    // paid order, issues a REAL Razorpay refund (the secret can't live in the\n    // browser). If the refund fails the order stays pending so we can retry.\n    try {\n      const res = await fetch(`/api/farmer/orders/${orderId}/decline`, {\n        method: 'POST',\n        credentials: 'same-origin',\n        headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },\n        body: JSON.stringify({ reason }),\n      })\n      const json = await res.json().catch(() => ({}))\n      if (!res.ok) {\n        alert(json.error || 'Could not decline the order. Please try again.')\n        return\n      }\n      setPendingOrders((prev) => prev.filter((o) => o.id !== orderId))\n      setDecliningOrder(null)\n    } catch {\n      alert('Network error. Please try again.')\n    } finally {\n      setProcessingOrderId(null)\n    }\n  }"
}

I'll add idempotency two ways: a key on order placement (so a double-tap or lost-response retry can't create duplicate orders or double-decrement stock), and reuse of the existing Razorpay order on retry (so a buyer can't be charged twice). Migration first:

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_01TJG1LvWBFgQBWuMfViB9Hh
```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/veerendra/farmer/yourfamilyfarmer/scripts/idempotency-migration.sql",
  "content": "-- ================================================================\n-- YFF Idempotency migration  (feature #3 — duplicate order/payment guard)\n-- Run in: Supabase Dashboard → SQL Editor → New Query → Run\n-- Safe to re-run (idempotent).\n-- ================================================================\n--\n-- On slow 4G a buyer may double-tap \"Place order\", or the response may be\n-- lost and the app retries. The client sends one idempotency_key per\n-- checkout attempt; if rows with that key already exist we return them\n-- instead of inserting a second set (and decrementing stock twice).\n--\n-- All rows from a single cart share the same key, so this is a plain index,\n-- not a unique constraint.\n\nALTER TABLE orders ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS idempotency_key text;\n\nCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS orders_idempotency_key_idx\n  ON orders(idempotency_key)\n  WHERE idempotency_key IS NOT NULL;\n"
}
```

Please log in again.', 401)\n\n  // Idempotency: if this checkout attempt was already saved (double-tap or a\n  // retry after a lost response), return the existing rows instead of placing\n  // a second order and decrementing stock again.\n  if (idempotencyKey) {\n    const { data: existing } = await supabase\n      .from('orders')\n      .select('id, order_code, total_price, delivery_fee')\n      .eq('idempotency_key', idempotencyKey)\n      .eq('consumer_id', consumer.id)\n    if (existing && existing.length > 0) {\n      const existingTotal = existing.reduce((s, o) => s + (Number(o.total_price) || 0), 0)\n      const existingFee = existing.reduce((s, o) => s + (Number(o.delivery_fee) || 0), 0)\n      return NextResponse.json({\n        ok: true,\n        orderIds: existing.map((r) => r.id),\n        orderCodes: existing.map((r) => (r as { order_code?: string | null }).order_code).filter(Boolean),\n        total: existingTotal,\n        deliveryFee: existingFee,\n        grandTotal: existingTotal + existingFee,\n        deduplicated: true,\n      })\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Farmer COD acceptance check"
}

Kept until the order\n  // actually saves, so a retry after a dropped response reuses the same key\n  // and the server returns the existing order instead of placing a new one.\n  const idempotencyKeys = useRef<Record<string, string>>({})\n  const getIdempotencyKey = (farmerId: string): string => {\n    if (!idempotencyKeys.current[farmerId]) {\n      idempotencyKeys.current[farmerId] =\n        (typeof crypto !== 'undefined' && crypto.randomUUID)\n          ? crypto.randomUUID()\n          : `${Date.now()}-${Math.random().toString(16).slice(2)}`\n    }\n    return idempotencyKeys.current[farmerId]\n  }"
}

52	// Recompute the Checkout signature and compare in constant time. Razorpay
53	// signs `${order_id}|${payment_id}` with HMAC-SHA256 keyed by the secret.
54	// A matching signature is the only proof the browser callback is genuine —
55	// never trust a client-reported "success" without this.
56	export function verifyPaymentSignature(args: {
57	  razorpayOrderId: string
58	  razorpayPaymentId: string
59	  signature: string
60	}): boolean {
61	  const expected = createHmac('sha256', getRazorpaySecret())
62	    .update(`${args.razorpayOrderId}|${args.razorpayPaymentId}`)
63	    .digest('hex')
64	  const a = Buffer.from(expected)
65	  const b = Buffer.from(args.signature)
66	  if (a.length !== b.length) return false
67	  return timingSafeEqual(a, b)
68	}
69

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/veerendra/farmer/yourfamilyfarmer/src/lib/razorpay.ts",
  "old_string": "  const a = Buffer.from(expected)\n  const b = Buffer.from(args.signature)\n  if (a.length !== b.length) return false\n  return timingSafeEqual(a, b)\n}",
  "new_string": "  const a = Buffer.from(expected)\n  const b = Buffer.from(args.signature)\n  if (a.length !== b.length) return false\n  return timingSafeEqual(a, b)\n}\n\n// Verify a Razorpay WEBHOOK signature. Webhooks are signed with the webhook\n// secret (configured in the dashboard, separate from the API key secret):\n// HMAC-SHA256 over the raw request body, hex-encoded. We must verify against\n// the EXACT bytes received, so the route reads req.text() before parsing.\nexport function verifyWebhookSignature(rawBody: string, signature: string | null | undefined): boolean {\n  const secret = process.env.RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET\n  if (!secret) throw new Error('RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET is not set.')\n  if (!signature) return false\n  const expected = createHmac('sha256', secret).update(rawBody).digest('hex')\n  const a = Buffer.from(expected)\n  const b = Buffer.from(signature)\n  if (a.length !== b.length) return false\n  return timingSafeEqual(a, b)\n}\n\n// Fetch all payment attempts Razorpay recorded against one order. Used by\n// reconciliation to decide whether a \"pending\" order was actually paid.\nexport async function fetchOrderPayments(orderId: string): Promise<Array<{ id: string; status: string }>> {\n  const res = await getRazorpayClient().orders.fetchPayments(orderId)\n  const items = (res as { items?: Array<{ id: string; status: string }> }).items ?? []\n  return items.map((p) => ({ id: String(p.id), status: String(p.status) }))\n}"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/veerendra/farmer/yourfamilyfarmer/src/app/api/orders/razorpay/webhook/route.ts",
  "content": "import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'\nimport { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'\nimport { verifyWebhookSignature } from '@/lib/razorpay'\n\nexport const runtime = 'nodejs'\nexport const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'\n\n// Razorpay → our server, independent of the buyer's browser. This is the\n// authoritative payment confirmation: if the buyer's phone dies right after\n// paying, the browser-side /verify never runs, but this webhook still marks\n// the order paid. Configure it in the Razorpay dashboard:\n//   Settings → Webhooks → add URL .../api/orders/razorpay/webhook\n//   events: payment.captured, payment.failed  (+ a webhook secret)\n// and set RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET to that secret.\n//\n// We always answer 200 once the signature is valid so Razorpay stops\n// retrying; processing failures are logged, not surfaced to Razorpay.\nexport async function POST(req: NextRequest) {\n  // Read the RAW body first — signature is computed over these exact bytes.\n  const raw = await req.text()\n  const signature = req.headers.get('x-razorpay-signature')\n\n  let valid: boolean\n  try {\n    valid = verifyWebhookSignature(raw, signature)\n  } catch (e) {\n    console.error('[YFF] webhook secret missing/misconfigured:', e)\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Webhook not configured.' }, { status: 500 })\n  }\n  if (!valid) {\n    console.warn('[YFF] razorpay webhook signature INVALID')\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid signature.' }, { status: 400 })\n  }\n\n  let event: {\n    event?: string\n    payload?: { payment?: { entity?: { id?: string; order_id?: string } } }\n  }\n  try {\n    event = JSON.parse(raw)\n  } catch {\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Bad JSON.' }, { status: 400 })\n  }\n\n  const supabase = createClient(\n    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,\n    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,\n  )\n\n  const payment = event.payload?.payment?.entity\n  const razorpayOrderId = payment?.order_id\n  const razorpayPaymentId = payment?.id\n\n  try {\n    if (event.event === 'payment.captured' && razorpayOrderId) {\n      // Mark paid only if not already — keeps the webhook idempotent against\n      // Razorpay's retries and against a browser /verify that already ran.\n      const { error } = await supabase\n        .from('orders')\n        .update({ payment_status: 'paid', razorpay_payment_id: razorpayPaymentId ?? null })\n        .eq('razorpay_order_id', razorpayOrderId)\n        .neq('payment_status', 'paid')\n      if (error) console.error('[YFF] webhook captured update failed:', error.message)\n    } else if (event.event === 'payment.failed' && razorpayOrderId) {\n      const { error } = await supabase\n        .from('orders')\n        .update({ payment_status: 'failed' })\n        .eq('razorpay_order_id', razorpayOrderId)\n        .eq('payment_status', 'pending')\n      if (error) console.error('[YFF] webhook failed update failed:', error.message)\n    }\n    // Unknown events are acknowledged and ignored.\n  } catch (e) {\n    console.error('[YFF] webhook processing error:', e)\n  }\n\n  return NextResponse.json({ ok: true })\n}\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/veerendra/farmer/yourfamilyfarmer/src/app/api/cron/reconcile-payments/route.ts",
  "content": "import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'\nimport { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'\nimport { fetchOrderPayments } from '@/lib/razorpay'\n\nexport const runtime = 'nodejs'\nexport const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'\n\n// Safety net for the rare order that stays \"pending\" after a payment — e.g.\n// the buyer paid but both the browser /verify AND the webhook were missed.\n// Runs on a Vercel cron (see vercel.json). For each pending Razorpay order\n// older than 15 minutes we ask Razorpay what really happened and mark it\n// paid if a payment was captured.\n//\n// Authorised by CRON_SECRET: Vercel automatically sends it as a Bearer token\n// when the CRON_SECRET env var is set, so external callers can't trigger it.\nconst STALE_MINUTES = 15\nconst BATCH_LIMIT = 100\n\nexport async function GET(req: NextRequest) {\n  const secret = process.env.CRON_SECRET\n  if (secret) {\n    const auth = req.headers.get('authorization')\n    if (auth !== `Bearer ${secret}`) {\n      return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Unauthorized.' }, { status: 401 })\n    }\n  }\n\n  const supabase = createClient(\n    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,\n    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,\n  )\n\n  const cutoff = new Date(Date.now() - STALE_MINUTES * 60 * 1000).toISOString()\n\n  const { data: orders, error } = await supabase\n    .from('orders')\n    .select('id, razorpay_order_id, created_at')\n    .eq('payment_method', 'razorpay')\n    .eq('payment_status', 'pending')\n    .not('razorpay_order_id', 'is', null)\n    .lt('created_at', cutoff)\n    .limit(BATCH_LIMIT)\n\n  if (error) {\n    console.error('[YFF] reconcile query failed:', error.message)\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: error.message }, { status: 500 })\n  }\n\n  // Distinct Razorpay order ids (a cart shares one across its rows).\n  const orderIds = [...new Set((orders ?? []).map((o) => o.razorpay_order_id as string).filter(Boolean))]\n\n  let reconciled = 0\n  const errors: string[] = []\n\n  for (const rzpId of orderIds) {\n    try {\n      const payments = await fetchOrderPayments(rzpId)\n      const captured = payments.find((p) => p.status === 'captured')\n      if (captured) {\n        const { error: updErr } = await supabase\n          .from('orders')\n          .update({ payment_status: 'paid', razorpay_payment_id: captured.id })\n          .eq('razorpay_order_id', rzpId)\n          .neq('payment_status', 'paid')\n        if (updErr) errors.push(`${rzpId}: ${updErr.message}`)\n        else reconciled += 1\n      }\n    } catch (e) {\n      errors.push(`${rzpId}: ${(e as Error).message}`)\n    }\n  }\n\n  return NextResponse.json({\n    ok: true,\n    checked: orderIds.length,\n    reconciled,\n    errors: errors.length ? errors : undefined,\n  })\n}\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Good question — and you're right: the **Razorpay dashboard belongs to your client**, so the dashboard part (Step 3) is **their** job. Let me explain what each piece is, why it matters, and exactly who does what.

## First, what is a "webhook" and why do we need it?

Think of a payment like a courier delivery:

- **Right now**, when a customer pays, their *phone* tells your server "payment done." But if their phone dies, loses signal, or they close the app right after paying — **that message never arrives.** Result: money left their account, but your app thinks the order isn't paid. Angry customer. 😡
- **A webhook** is Razorpay calling your server **directly** ("Hey, this order was paid"), separate from the customer's phone. Razorpay never loses signal. So the order always gets marked paid, even if the customer's phone explodes. ✅

That's the whole point: **it makes payments reliable.** It's the single most important trust feature.

## What are the two "secrets"?

**1. `RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET`** — a password shared between Razorpay and your server.
- When Razorpay calls your server, how does your server know it's *really* Razorpay and not a hacker faking "payment done" to get free produce? They both share this secret password. Your server checks it on every call. No match = ignored.
- **The same secret value must be in two places:** the client's Razorpay dashboard AND your Vercel.

**2. `CRON_SECRET`** — has **nothing to do with Razorpay or your client.** It's just a random password *you* invent, to protect your daily "double-check payments" job from being triggered by strangers. You set this yourself.

## Who does what — clearly divided

| Step | Who | What |
|---|---|---|
| Invent the webhook secret | **You or client** (agree on one value) | e.g. `yff_whsec_8392kdp23` — any random string |
| Create the webhook in Razorpay dashboard | **🧑‍💼 CLIENT** (it's their account) | Add the URL + events + paste the secret |
| Put `RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET` in Vercel | **👨‍💻 YOU** | Same secret value the client used |
| Put `CRON_SECRET` in Vercel | **👨‍💻 YOU** | Any random string you make up |

## Message you can forward to your client

> Hi, for reliable payment confirmation we need a **webhook** set up in your Razorpay dashboard. Please:
> 1. Go to **Razorpay Dashboard → Settings → Webhooks → Add New Webhook**
> 2. **URL:** `https://your-family-farmer.vercel.app/api/orders/razorpay/webhook`  *(I'll send you the exact live URL)*
> 3. **Secret:** use this value → `__________`  *(we'll agree on one)*
> 4. **Active events:** tick **`payment.captured`** and **`payment.failed`**
> 5. Save. Then send me a screenshot confirming it's active.
>
> This makes sure that if a customer's phone disconnects right after paying, the order still gets confirmed automatically.

---

## So what should you do *right now*?

The webhook (Steps 2's first var + Step 3) needs the client, which may take a day or two. **But that should NOT block you.** Here's the key point:

👉 **Everything else works without the webhook.** The webhook is an *extra safety layer*. Payments, refunds, order IDs, and idempotency all work fine on their own.

So my recommendation:

1. **You do now:** run the 3 SQL files (Step 1) + add just `CRON_SECRET` to Vercel (your own random string). Skip `RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET` for now.
2. **I push** → you test order IDs + refunds (these don't need the webhook).
3. **Client sets up the webhook** whenever they can. The moment they do and you add `RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET` to Vercel, the safety layer switches on automatically — no code change needed.

Want me to go ahead on that plan? Run the 3 SQL files, tell me when done, and I'll push. (The webhook will simply sit dormant and harmless until the client finishes their part.)

  "content": "# 🧑‍💼 Razorpay Webhook Setup — For the Client\n\n> One small setup task in **your Razorpay dashboard**. Takes ~5 minutes.\n\n## Why we need this (the use)\n\nWhen a customer pays, their **phone** normally tells our server \"payment done.\"\nBut if their phone dies, loses internet, or they close the app right after\npaying, **that message never arrives** — the money leaves their account, but the\norder shows as unpaid. Angry customer, and a refund headache.\n\nA **webhook** fixes this: Razorpay calls our server **directly** to confirm the\npayment, completely independent of the customer's phone. Razorpay never loses\nsignal, so **every paid order gets confirmed automatically.**\n\n👉 In short: **this makes payments reliable and prevents \"money deducted but\norder not placed\" complaints.**\n\n---\n\n## What to do (step by step)\n\n1. Log in to your **Razorpay Dashboard**.\n2. Go to **Settings → Webhooks → Add New Webhook**.\n3. Fill in:\n   - **Webhook URL:**\n     ```\n     https://YOUR-REAL-DOMAIN/api/orders/razorpay/webhook\n     ```\n     *(your developer will give you the exact live URL)*\n   - **Secret:**\n     ```\n     __________\n     ```\n     *(a password — agree on one value with your developer; the same value\n     must be used on both sides)*\n   - **Active Events:** tick these two only:\n     - ✅ `payment.captured`\n     - ✅ `payment.failed`\n4. Click **Save / Create Webhook**.\n5. Check that it shows as **Active**, and send your developer a **screenshot**\n   confirming it.\n\n---\n\n## ✅ That's it\n\nOnce this is active and your developer plugs the same secret into the website,\npayments will confirm automatically and reliably — even if a customer's phone\ndisconnects the moment after paying.\n"
}

> AGENT

Good question — let me clear up the confusion, because these two are **not** the same as the Razorpay API keys.

## The Razorpay API keys — already done ✅
The `rzp_live_...` key + secret that came from your client are **already in Vercel** (that's why your test payment worked). You don't need anything new there. Those are the only "keys from the client."

## The two NEW env vars — neither is an API key from the client

**1. `RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET` — NOT a key you request from the client.**
This is a password that **gets created during the webhook setup**, not something that already exists in their account. When the client adds the webhook (Step 4 in the guide), there's a **"Secret" box they type into**. Whatever they type there *becomes* the webhook secret.

So there's no "give me the webhook key" — instead, **you both agree on one value.** Two ways:
- **You suggest it:** "Use `yff_whsec_8392kdp23` as the webhook secret" → client types that → you put the same in Vercel. *(Simplest.)*
- **Client picks it:** they type their own → they tell you the value → you put it in Vercel.

Either way it's just **one agreed password**, shared between their dashboard and your Vercel. Not a pre-existing key.

**2. `CRON_SECRET` — nothing from the client at all.**
You invent any random string yourself. The client never sees it. It only guards your own daily payment-check job.

## So, to summarize

| Value | From the client? |
|---|---|
| Razorpay API key + secret (`rzp_live_...`) | ✅ Yes — **already have it, in Vercel** |
| `RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET` | ❌ No — a password you + client **agree on** during webhook setup |
| `CRON_SECRET` | ❌ No — you invent it yourself |

**Bottom line:** you don't need to request any new keys from the client. You just need to **agree on one webhook password** with them — and even that can wait, since the webhook is the optional safety layer. You can run Step 1 + add `CRON_SECRET` and deploy everything else right now.

Want me to push once you've done Step 1 (SQL) + `CRON_SECRET`?

300	function DeliveryPanel({ order }: { order: Order }) {
301	  const ds: DeliveryStatus = (order.delivery_status as DeliveryStatus) || 'unassigned'
302	
303	  const steps: Array<{ key: DeliveryStatus; label: string; sub: string; at: string | null | undefined }> = [
304	    { key: 'unassigned', label: 'Order placed', sub: 'Waiting for a delivery boy', at: order.created_at },
305	    { key: 'assigned', label: 'Rider assigned', sub: 'On the way to farmer', at: order.assigned_at },
306	    { key: 'picked_up', label: 'Picked up', sub: 'Collected from farmer', at: order.picked_up_at },
307	    { key: 'out_for_delivery', label: 'Out for delivery', sub: 'On the way to you', at: order.out_for_delivery_at },
308	    { key: 'delivered', label: 'Delivered', sub: 'Order completed', at: order.delivered_at },
309	  ]
310	
311	  const stageIndex = (k: DeliveryStatus) => ['unassigned', 'assigned', 'picked_up', 'out_for_delivery', 'delivered'].indexOf(k)
312	  const current = stageIndex(ds)
313	
314	  const formatAt = (iso: string | null | undefined) => {
315	    if (!iso) return ''
316	    const d = new Date(iso)
317	    return d.toLocaleString('en-IN', { day: 'numeric', month: 'short', hour: '2-digit', minute: '2-digit' })
318	  }
319	
320	  return (
321	    <div className="bg-white rounded-2xl border border-gray-100 p-4 space-y-4">
322	      <div className="flex items-center gap-2">
323	        <span className="text-base">🛵</span>
324	        <p className="text-xs font-bold text-gray-500 uppercase tracking-wide">
325	          Home delivery / ఇంటికి డెలివరీ
326	        </p>
327	      </div>
328	
329	      {/* Handover OTP — visible once a rider has been assigned. Customer
330	          reads this 4-digit code to the rider at the door so the system
331	          can confirm the right person received the goods. */}
332	      {ds !== 'unassigned' && ds !== 'delivered' && order.handover_otp && (
333	        <div className="bg-amber-50 border-2 border-amber-300 rounded-2xl px-4 py-3 text-center">
334	          <p className="text-[10px] font-bold text-amber-800 uppercase tracking-wide">
335	            Read this code to the delivery boy at your door
336	          </p>
337	          <p className="text-[10px] text-amber-700 mt-0.5">
338	            డోర్ వద్ద డెలివరీ బాయ్‌కు ఈ కోడ్ చెప్పండి
339	          </p>
340	          <p className="text-4xl font-black tracking-widest text-amber-900 mt-2 font-mono">
341	            {order.handover_otp}
342	          </p>
343	        </div>
344	      )}
345	
346	      {ds === 'delivered' && (
347	        <div className="bg-green-50 border border-green-200 rounded-2xl px-4 py-3 text-center">
348	          <p className="text-sm font-extrabold text-green-800">✓ Delivered</p>
349	          <p className="text-xs text-green-700 mt-0.5">{formatAt(order.delivered_at)}</p>
350	        </div>
351	      )}
352	
353	      {/* Status timeline */}
354	      <ol className="space-y-2">
355	        {steps.map((s, idx) => {
356	          const reached = idx <= current
357	          const isCurrent = idx === current
358	          return (
359	            <li key={s.key} className="flex items-start gap-3">
360	              <div className="flex flex-col items-center pt-0.5">
361	                <span className={`w-3 h-3 rounded-full ${reached ? 'bg-green-700' : 'bg-gray-200'} ${isCurrent ? 'ring-2 ring-green-200' : ''}`} />
362	                {idx < steps.length - 1 && (
363	                  <span className={`w-0.5 flex-1 mt-0.5 ${idx < current ? 'bg-green-700' : 'bg-gray-200'}`} style={{ minHeight: 14 }} />
364	                )}
365	              </div>
366	              <div className="flex-1 pb-1">
367	                <p className={`text-xs font-bold ${reached ? 'text-gray-900' : 'text-gray-400'}`}>{s.label}</p>
368	                <p className="text-[10px] text-gray-500 leading-snug">{s.sub}</p>
369	                {s.at && reached && (
370	                  <p className="text-[10px] text-gray-400 mt-0.5">{formatAt(s.at)}</p>
371	                )}
372	              </div>
373	            </li>
374	          )
375	        })}
376	      </ol>
377	
378	      {/* Delivery address (always visible — confirms what consumer entered) */}

  "new_string": "          >\n            📞 Call · {order.rider.phone}\n          </a>\n        </div>\n      )}\n    </div>\n  )\n}\n\n// Refund timeline. Maps the stored refund_status (our manual 'initiated', or\n// a Razorpay status: 'pending' / 'processed' / 'failed') onto a simple\n// buyer-facing progression: Initiated → Processing → Credited.\nfunction RefundPanel({ order }: { order: Order }) {\n  const rs = (order.refund_status || '').toLowerCase()\n  const failed = rs === 'failed'\n  const amount = order.refund_amount ?? order.total_price ?? 0\n\n  // current = how far along the 3-step bar we are.\n  // initiated → 0, pending → 1, processed → 2 (credited).\n  const current = rs === 'processed' ? 2 : rs === 'pending' ? 1 : 0\n\n  const steps = [\n    { label: 'Refund initiated', sub: 'We started your refund' },\n    { label: 'Processing', sub: 'Sent to your bank/UPI' },\n    { label: 'Credited', sub: 'Reflects in 3–5 business days' },\n  ]\n\n  const refundedAt = order.refunded_at\n    ? new Date(order.refunded_at).toLocaleString('en-IN', { day: 'numeric', month: 'short', hour: '2-digit', minute: '2-digit' })\n    : null\n\n  if (failed) {\n    return (\n      <div className=\"bg-red-50 border border-red-200 rounded-2xl p-4 space-y-1.5\">\n        <span className=\"inline-block text-[10px] font-bold px-2 py-0.5 rounded-full bg-red-100 text-red-800\">\n          ⚠️ Refund failed / రీఫండ్ విఫలమైంది\n        </span>\n        <p className=\"text-sm text-red-800 leading-snug\">\n          Your refund of ₹{amount} could not be processed. Please contact support and we&apos;ll fix it.\n        </p>\n      </div>\n    )\n  }\n\n  return (\n    <div className=\"bg-purple-50 border border-purple-200 rounded-2xl p-4 space-y-3\">\n      <div className=\"flex items-center justify-between gap-2\">\n        <span className=\"inline-block text-[10px] font-bold px-2 py-0.5 rounded-full bg-purple-100 text-purple-800\">\n          💸 Refund of ₹{amount}\n        </span>\n        {refundedAt && <span className=\"text-[10px] text-purple-500\">{refundedAt}</span>}\n      </div>\n\n      <ol className=\"space-y-2\">\n        {steps.map((s, idx) => {\n          const reached = idx <= current\n          const isCurrent = idx === current\n          return (\n            <li key={s.label} className=\"flex items-start gap-3\">\n              <div className=\"flex flex-col items-center pt-0.5\">\n                <span className={`w-3 h-3 rounded-full ${reached ? 'bg-purple-600' : 'bg-purple-200'} ${isCurrent ? 'ring-2 ring-purple-200' : ''}`} />\n                {idx < steps.length - 1 && (\n                  <span className={`w-0.5 mt-0.5 ${idx < current ? 'bg-purple-600' : 'bg-purple-200'}`} style={{ minHeight: 14 }} />\n                )}\n              </div>\n              <div className=\"flex-1 pb-1\">\n                <p className={`text-xs font-bold ${reached ? 'text-purple-900' : 'text-purple-300'}`}>{s.label}</p>\n                <p className=\"text-[10px] text-purple-500 leading-snug\">{s.sub}</p>\n              </div>\n            </li>\n          )\n        })}\n      </ol>\n\n      {order.refund_id && (\n        <p className=\"text-[10px] text-purple-400 font-mono border-t border-purple-100 pt-2\">Ref: {order.refund_id}</p>\n      )}\n    </div>\n  )\n}"
}
